My family and I have been homeless since 2018—nearly seven years of relentless hardship, violence, institutional negligence, and false promises.

During this time, we have endured countless attacks, been subjected to unlawful evictions, and lost nearly everything we hold dear, including irreplaceable personal items, beloved pets, and even the ashes of a deceased family member.

This suffering has not been accidental.

We have been dehumanized, ignored, and treated as an inconvenience to be removed rather than as human beings in need of help.

What makes this betrayal even more devastating is that it was enabled—if not directly orchestrated—by organizations that publicly claim to support the homeless.

This letter is not just a plea for help.

We urge you to investigate New Hope C.O.R.P.S. for their unethical practices and their participation in the illegal eviction that destroyed our lives.

Behind that facade, however, lies a far more sinister reality.

They collected our information, accepted our paperwork, and gave us hope that a resolution was within reach.

Each time we followed up, we were met with excuses: processing delays, misplaced files, or simply, “It's coming soon.” We trusted them because we had no reason not to.

We believed, as anyone would, that an organization with their stated mission would act in good faith.

But this was a facade.

They knew exactly where to find us because we had given them that information in good faith.

This was not public property—it was privately owned land, making the notice legally invalid.

Hours later, when we stopped to rest after moving heavy items, another officer declared the move over, citing "trespassing," and left the remaining belongings to be crushed in a dumpster.

The destruction was not incidental.

It was the culmination of years of systemic neglect and violence.

We have been attacked repeatedly, with one individual—Gay Calderon—carrying out more than twenty assaults against us.

Juan sat there, motionless, absorbing yet another betrayal.

He just sat with that silent resignation that only comes after years of learning that hope is just another kind of trap. 5. **The Illegal Eviction: The Final Knife Twist** In January 2025, a sergeant from MDPD came to our camp under the pretense of outreach.

He asked for names, dates, and personal information.

He said it was to help New Hope finalize our case.

We gave him everything.

Days later, Stephen Heath showed up with a five-day eviction notice taped to a tree.

We were on private land.

Stephen shrugged and said, *"Don't worry about it.

Nothing’s going to happen."* Two days later, MDPD arrived with multiple officers and a Chase Bank-hired worker to execute the eviction.

New Hope stood alongside them in silence.

The worker didn’t "move" our belongings; he looted them.

He opened boxes and pocketed items.

We caught him trying to steal Juan’s locked chest.

After hours of labor, we paused to catch our breath.

That was when Officer Cheney declared us trespassers and ordered everything left behind destroyed.

The compactor's jaws ground through what remained of our life: clothes, documents, photographs.

We watched it all turn into rubble.

Targeting someone based on their homeless status is a hate crime.

What happened to us wasn't an accident or a misunderstanding.

It was systematic, targeted, and malicious.

It requires proof of targeting based on class status.

New Hope’s entire approach—deceptive outreach, false promises, coordinated eviction—centers on the fact that we are homeless.

Trucks roar by all night long, the wind from their passage threatening to pull us under their wheels.

Our pets, once our last source of comfort, have been killed or disappeared.

One kitten was found with its skull crushed on the pavement.

We try not to think about how it happened.

We try to move forward.

Now the country belongs to their cowardly offspring.

People who hide behind bureaucracy.

Investigate New Hope.

Demand accountability.

Restore some measure of trust to a community that has been systematically betrayed.

Because silence got us here.

And if that silence continues, it will only get worse.

Thank you for your time and attention.

People stayed silent, and that silence allowed the rot to spread unchecked.

Silence doesn’t require empathy or courage.

Silence lets you believe the problem is someone else's to solve.

In that time, we've faced unimaginable violence, systemic neglect, and a pattern of deception so calculated it would almost be impressive if it weren’t destroying lives.

I don't believe the Homeless Trust is aware of the extent of New Hope’s indiscretions.

If they did, they’d have been more careful.

Because make no mistake: what they did to us wasn't just cruel or unethical.

It doesn’t provide loopholes for those who claim good intentions while systematically betraying the most vulnerable people in the community.

What follows is a patient, careful reconstruction of how New Hope used hope itself as a weapon against us.

It's about the people who smiled as they set us up.

The promises made and broken.

The laws bent and ignored.

The lives they treated like debris to be swept away. --- ### **The Instances of False Hope: A Step-by-Step Betrayal** New Hope didn’t fail us by accident.

Their methods weren't sophisticated—they didn’t have to be.

They knew we were desperate.

And they knew that, until now, no one would speak up.

Here’s how they did it: 1. **Dee-Dee: The Original Hook** Dee-Dee was the first.

She arrived one day with Belen, armed with smiles and confident promises.

She said New Hope was different.

She promised to get me into a Suboxone program to help me maintain my sobriety.

She said we were "priority cases." We believed every word.

Her requests seemed reasonable at first: documents, signatures, a bit of patience.

We provided everything without hesitation.

And while we waited, Dee-Dee sent Belen to question other homeless individuals about my sobriety—prying into my personal medical history through gossip rather than speaking to me directly.

When I reached out to Dee-Dee for the Suboxone she promised, she ignored me.

Days stretched into weeks.

I messaged, called, and left voicemails.

My sobriety depended on that medication.

Without it, the inevitable happened: I relapsed.

I can't overstate the weight of that failure.

Sobriety is a fragile, uphill climb even under ideal circumstances.

When a person trusted with your recovery disappears without explanation, the fall feels inevitable.

When you're homeless, that fall is more like a freefall into an abyss.

And then one day, Dee-Dee was gone.

Just another person who dangled hope like bait and vanished when we reached for it. 2. **Adam: The Cleanup Man** Adam came next.

He said Dee-Dee had botched everything and promised to clean up the mess. *"Don't lose faith in us,"* he said. *"We'll make this right."* He spoke like someone who believed his own words.

He begged us to give New Hope another chance, and we did.

Seven months passed with no movement.

Just more vague assurances when we could get ahold of him—an increasingly rare event.

When we finally cornered him, Adam didn’t even remember who we were.

He promised to meet us twice to get things back on track.

We showed up both times.

He never did. 3. **Stephen Heath: The Illusion of Progress** Enter Stephen Heath.

He wasn’t even an outreach worker, yet somehow he was assigned to us.

Stephen eventually referred us to Taylor, the supposed gatekeeper of these vouchers.

When we contacted her, she responded with confusion: *"Who?

Never heard of it."* I posted about this absurd exchange on Facebook, trying to make sense of the contradiction.

Taylor removed the post without comment.

We even heard New Hope confirm the bed’s availability over the phone.

The next morning, we tried to finalize the details.

No one knew what we were talking about.

There never had been.

Juan sat there, motionless, absorbing yet another betrayal.

He just sat with that silent resignation that only comes after years of learning that hope is just another kind of trap. 5. **The Illegal Eviction: The Final Knife Twist** In January 2025, a sergeant from MDPD came to our camp under the pretense of outreach.

He asked for names, dates, and personal information.

He said it was to help New Hope finalize our case.

We gave him everything.

Days later, Stephen Heath showed up with a five-day eviction notice taped to a tree.

After hours of labor, we paused to catch our breath.

That was when Officer Cheney declared us trespassers and ordered everything left behind destroyed.

Silence doesn’t require empathy or courage.

Silence lets you believe the problem is someone else's to solve.

In that time, we've faced unimaginable violence, systemic neglect, and a pattern of deception so calculated it would almost be impressive if it weren’t destroying lives.

They said the right things.

They made the right promises.

We gave them everything: our documents, our time, our trust.

And for a while, it felt like something might finally shift in our favor.

For a while, we believed.

We didn’t know what was really happening behind the scenes.

I don't believe the Homeless Trust is aware of the extent of New Hope’s indiscretions.

I can’t believe you’d fund an organization that repeatedly failed us with the kind of casual indifference you'd expect from an understaffed DMV, not a supposedly compassionate outreach program.

If they did, they’d have been more careful.

Nothing’s going to happen."* Two days later, MDPD arrived with multiple officers and a Chase Bank-hired worker to execute the eviction.

He opened boxes and pocketed items.

We caught him trying to steal Juan’s locked chest.

After hours of labor, we paused to catch our breath.

That was when Officer Cheney declared us trespassers and ordered everything left behind destroyed.

The compactor's jaws ground through what remained of our life: clothes, documents, photographs.

We watched it all turn into rubble.

Later, when I messaged Stephen to tell him how betrayed we felt, he showed up with William and yelled at me: *"Stop blaming others for your problems."* The mask was gone.

New Hope wasn't just negligent.

Targeting someone based on their homeless status is a hate crime.

What happened to us wasn't an accident or a misunderstanding.

It was systematic, targeted, and malicious.

Throughout this period, we have placed our trust in organizations purportedly designed to assist individuals in our situation.

Chief among these is New Hope C.O.R.P.S., an entity we believed would be our advocate and guide toward stability.

Regrettably, our experiences have unveiled a pattern of negligence, deception, and rights violations that have not only deepened our plight but have also eroded our faith in the support systems meant to protect us. **Background on New Hope C.O.R.P.S.** Established in 1993, New Hope C.O.R.P.S. is a nonprofit, CARF-accredited community-based organization.

Their mission encompasses providing free substance abuse treatment, behavioral health services, HIV/AIDS prevention, testing and counseling, in-home counseling, and case management.

Trusting their assurances, we provided all necessary documentation and awaited the promised assistance.

However, instead of direct communication, Dee-Dee resorted to inquiring about my sobriety through third parties, infringing upon my privacy and potentially violating HIPAA regulations.

Subsequently, she became unresponsive to my urgent requests for Suboxone, leading to a relapse due to the abrupt discontinuation of support. 2. **Interaction with Adam** Following Dee-Dee's unexplained departure, Adam stepped in, acknowledging previous missteps and pledging to rectify them.

Despite his assurances, seven months elapsed without any tangible progress.

When we finally reconnected, Adam appeared unfamiliar with our case, mistakenly identifying us as new clients seeking rehabilitation services.

Promises of meetings were made but never honored, further exacerbating our sense of abandonment. 3. **Engagement with Stephen Heath and Taylor** Stephen Heath was subsequently assigned to our case, despite not being an outreach worker.

Attempts to highlight these discrepancies publicly were suppressed, as evidenced by the removal of my social media posts detailing our experiences. **The Illegal Eviction** In January 2025, our situation took a dire turn.

We’ve been homeless for seven years now, not because of drugs or crime, but because of an endless cycle of systemic failures and cruel betrayals.

The relentless cycle of being promised a way out only to have it ripped away at the last second.

Instead, they gave us the runaround, lied, ignored us, and in the end, actively participated in an illegal eviction that left us worse off than we were before we met them.

Here’s a numbered list of the times they gave us hope, only to rip it away, leading to our inevitable conclusion that they were aptly renamed "No Hope." #### **5.

Dee-Dee and the First Round of Lies** When we first met Dee-Dee from New Hope, she was practically glowing with enthusiasm.

She *knew* the perfect place for us.

She *guaranteed* we were at the top of the list.

She even got me Suboxone, which led to a horrific withdrawal ordeal when it wasn’t managed properly.

But she was gone as soon as we needed her again.

And when we finally saw her in person after months of silence, she claimed she never got any of our messages—a technological impossibility.

Then she told us to "just go to HAC," washing her hands of us entirely. #### **4.

Adam’s Grand Redemption Arc** After we had long given up on New Hope, Adam showed up out of nowhere, acting like he was on a mission to redeem the organization.

He *begged* us to let him fix what Dee-Dee had destroyed.

Reluctantly, we gave him a chance.

Seven months later, when we called him, he acted like he didn’t know who we were.

He scheduled a meeting for the next day, didn’t show up.

Rescheduled for the following day, didn’t show up then either.

And that was the last we ever heard from Adam. #### **3.

Enter Stephen Heath** After complaining about Adam, they sent Stephen Heath.

He was the first one to truly seem like he had a handle on things.

We were skeptical but hopeful.

We even sent Stephen a text thanking him for his efforts, telling him he was the first person who had given us real hope.

He never replied, which struck me as odd.

And then, when he did show up again, all he said was, *"Don’t thank me yet."* At the time, we thought he was being humble.

Looking back, it was ominous. #### **2.

The Call with Taylor** When we waited past October 1st, 2024, and still hadn't heard anything, we texted Stephen.

He told us to call Taylor.

We assumed Taylor was our contact at HUD.

Finally, we thought, things were moving forward.

We were *excited* to make the call.

That excitement was crushed instantly.

Taylor was not a HUD representative—she was Stephen's supervisor.

And she had already decided we weren't getting housed.

Later, I found Taylor’s **LinkedIn profile**, where she bragged about her "passion for community engagement" and listed *rapid rehousing* as one of the programs she oversees.

I posted about it on New Hope’s Facebook page.

My post was deleted within the hour. #### **1.
